The last landing is N-SKILL-3's follow-up, `13f56c99`; Part I closed at the merge, and the Discord reply leg, on the owner's list at Part I's end, ran on `36f58761` (2026-11-01T05:22Z), so revoking both test bots' tokens is the owner's next step, with the LICENSE's holder line (*Owner actions still open*).

This file shows, for every task of the programme, the part it belongs to, what has been implemented (with a link to the requirement it answers), its status, the evidence for that status, and what remains open before the task or its part can close. It summarises `tasks/deps-upgrade/programme-plan.md` (rev 1.23, the plan of record) and never overrides it: every requirement link points there, and where the two differ the plan wins and this file is corrected.

The parts are the plan's own. **Part I** is the toolchain and dependency wave: the commits that moved no version (§3.1), the twenty pre-cut fixes, Phases A–L of §3.2 through P38, the FRESHNESS-SWEEP, E8-PROBE and the end-to-end gates E1–E3 (§4.5; E6 ran at the consent point, and E4, E5, E7 and E8 run with E1–E3 again at §3.3's step 19, the beta's gate, by the owner's ruling of 2026-10-30). **Part II** is the flexible apps, the Q entries of §3.3. **Part III** is the modernization, the M entries of §3.3. Within Part I the rows follow the plan's phases; within Parts II and III they follow §3.3's merged-order steps, and a step that carries both parts appears in each part's table with that part's ids alone. The ids of Parts II and III that the plan landed or placed inside Part I — Q00–Q03, Q15–Q17, M01–M03 and M44 in §3.1, Q11 and M63 in Phase A–B, M04 + M07 inside P36 — are counted in Part I's rows, so Parts II and III here are the after-P38 work alone.

The status words: **Complete** — every entry in the row has landed (a measurement: been taken) and its gate is recorded; **In progress** — some of it has landed, or a run of record is under way; **Not started** — nothing in the row has landed.

| Phase G — MCP client: N-MCP-1, N-MCP-3, N-MCP-4, P19 (prep), N-MCP-1b, P19b (prep) | [§3.2 Phase G](tasks/deps-upgrade/programme-plan.md) | N-MCP-1 (`f010f0a8`), its client half (Z5-02): `McpClient::call_tool` reconnects once **before** a send when no service is installed or its transport has closed — serialised by the new `ClientInner::reconnect_lock`, so two calls that find one dead child respawn it once — and never after one: a timeout, a server error, a transport error or a mid-call close goes back to the caller, so a non-idempotent tool no longer runs twice; one deadline (`request_timeout`) spans the pre-send reconnect and the call (N-MCP-2's residue closed); `install_service` no longer resets the attempt count, so a server that handshakes and then exits on every call reaches `ReconnectExhausted` instead of looping to the sandbox's cut; a timed-out or errored call's child is kept (only a closed transport is respawned), so a server that hangs on every call is no longer restarted in session; five new `client::call_tests`, two daemon tests written against the old ladder adapted to the pre-send form; `tasks/extension-enable-design.md` rev 20 (§3.6 item 1, §10 case 7(d), notes at §3.2 T4b and §3.6 item 3, the ledger half's notes and §13 Q8), `docs/tools/TECHNICAL.md`'s MCP bullet and two comments in `tools/mcp/classify.rs`; no version moved. The ledger half (T6(a)'s sub-choice) lands as N-MCP-1b, right after P19 (the lead's placement, reversible). N-MCP-3 (`6e241978`; Z5-04): `call_tool`, `list_tools`, `list_tools_once` and `ping` take the service lock only to clone the current service's `Peer` (`McpClient::current_peer`) and await the request on the clone, so two calls to one server run at once and a queued call no longer spends its deadline waiting for the lock; `reconnect` closes the old service after releasing the lock; a request a disable cuts reads `McpError::Closed` ("client sealed by disable"), not `TransportClosed` (`McpClient::call_error`); T3's drain confirmed to bound a call on a cloned peer (it counts the registry's `CallGuard`); a disable of a server hung in a call past the drain returns after the drain and the close (stdio: rmcp's graceful wait, up to 3 s) instead of at the call's own deadline; two `client::concurrency_tests` and two daemon tests; `tasks/extension-enable-design.md` rev 21 (§3.2 T4's bound, §8's disable latency, §3.6's reaper sentence, §3.3.1's reader-task sentence, notes at §3.2 T4b), two doc comments in `managers/mcp.rs` and `T4_BOUND`'s in `managers/extensions.rs`; no version moved; T4's detach warning keeps its words, which now name a cause that no longer occurs (presented, not changed). N-MCP-4 (`1cd9dc26`; Z5-05): a remote MCP tool registers as `bridge::mcp_tool_name(server, tool)` — every character of the tool's part outside `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` becomes `_`, with no collapsing, so a name already in the alphabet is unchanged, and the whole `<server>__<tool>` is capped at 64 (`PROVIDER_TOOL_NAME_MAX`; past it the tool's part ends in `_` and 8 hex digits of the raw name's SHA-256) — the alphabet both providers document (Anthropic `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,128}$`, OpenAI at most 64 characters) and the stricter cap; the backend keeps the raw name for the call; `bridge::map_tool_list` maps one server's list before registration, keeping the first of two tools that map to one name with a warning naming both, at E4 (`McpSupervisor::load`) and on the §3.7 refresh (`on_tool_list_changed`'s `incoming`, a second site the entry did not cite); four core tests, two daemon tests (`StubOpts::echo_name`); `docs/Daemon_Manual.md` (the mapped name is the capability; the combined name at most 64), `docs/Skill_Template_Reference.md`, `docs/tools/TECHNICAL.md` and the registry's 256-byte comment; no version moved; a template or skill naming a remapped tool by its raw name stops matching. P19 (`93463cc6`; DU-25 (s1), (v4)/(r1′), (p4), (m) (b); A1-04, A-02, A-03, A-04, A-27, Z5-01, Z5-03, Z5-13, Z5-14): `rmcp = "3.5.1"` (from `"0.16"`) and a direct `process-wrap = { version = "10.0.1", features = ["tokio1"] }` in `crates/openalpaca_mcp/Cargo.toml`; the lock rmcp and rmcp-macros 0.16.0 → 3.5.1 (two `--precise` step-backs from 3.8.0), process-wrap 9.1.1 → 10.0.1, darling 0.23.0 dropped; the 3.5 port (the research's rewrite of the prep `88394660`: constructors for the non-exhaustive model types, `ContentBlock` for `Content`/`RawContent`, the optional server identity, the five annotation helpers, and all 19 `ToolAnnotations` literals (core and the daemon's `routes/tools` tests) in the builder form); every connection through rmcp's `Auto` lifecycle (`server/discover` at `2026-07-28`, else `initialize` at `2025-11-25`); the stdio child as the leader of its own process group with `group::EndGroupWithLeader` over it, so a disable ends what the server started (a `setsid` escapee named, the walk (i)); DU-25 (m) (b): a per-server `protocol` key — `"auto"`, `"legacy"` or `"2026-07-28"` (`McpProtocol`, `skip_serializing_if`, every unpinned `config_fingerprint` unchanged), `FailureReason::ProtocolNegotiationFailed` (`protocol_negotiation_failed`, ZCode's word, actionable, its text naming `protocol = "legacy"`) for `NoCompatibleProtocolVersion`, `ExpectedInitResult`, `LegacyFallbackFailed`, a close during the probe and, under the pin, a probe refused as a legacy server refuses it (a `2026-07-28` server's own −32021 and −32020 stay `unreachable`, as under `auto`), a respawn opened in the era its load negotiated, a warning for `connect_timeout_secs` ≤ 10 under `auto`, a `get_info` override sending our `client_info` and no capabilities (the version `2025-11-25`, which only `Initialize` reads), and the row's `protocol_version`, `connect_ms` and `list_ms` (`GET /v1/extensions`, `openalpaca ext info`, the GUI types and a `protocol-mismatch` row); `tasks/extension-enable-design.md` rev 22, the Daemon and CLI Manuals, `API_MAP.md`, `docs/api`, CLAUDE.md's MCP bullets and `config/mcp.toml` row; the npx e2e pinned to `@modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem@1.0.0` (F-14); RUSTSEC-2026-0189 cleared. N-MCP-1b (`8803771f`; Z5-02; T6(a)'s sub-choice, taken 2026-10-19, design §13 Q8): the ledger learns a stdio server's exit at once — an exit observer on every stdio spawn (`ReportExit`, a process-wrap `CommandWrapper` stacked outermost over P19's process-group pair, told what the process's `wait()` returned), the client's `ConnectionEvent::{Exited, Reconnected}` to a receiver installed once at E2 (`McpClient::on_connection_event`; nothing reported once the client is sealed, so a disable, a reload, a failed bring-up or the shutdown is not an exit), and two ledger CASes at the load's generation, each announced once by the ledger itself: `mark_exited` (`Enabled → Failed{Crashed}`, the detail the exit status, e.g. `exit status: 3` or `signal: 15 (SIGTERM)`, not reaped — the tools stay registered and on the surface and the gate admits the next call, one predicate `ExtensionLedger::admits_calls` at the gate, the surface filter, `blocked_providers`, `withheld_name`, `name_lost` and `is_live_incumbent`) and `mark_recovered` (back to `Enabled` when that call's pre-send reconnect handshakes); `mark_failed` takes an exit's `Failed` whose reconnects ran out to the reaper; a declaration removed under an admitted `Failed` is torn down with T0 and the drain (the residue arm takes an `Enabled` snapshot a concurrent `mark_failed` turned `Failed`); a gate case the decision requires, the lead's reading, reversible; the reason word stays `crashed`; four daemon gate tests (`StubOpts::exit_on_trigger`), the crash test adapted (the row reads the exit, then recovers; the reaped failure never respawns), one stdio, one client, four ledger and one registry test; `tasks/extension-enable-design.md` rev 23, the Daemon and GUI Manuals, `docs/tools/TECHNICAL.md`, `docs/api`; CLAUDE.md's two MCP bullets brought current (49,999 bytes); no version moved. P19b (`88984fff`; D5 (a) with its three conditions; A1-12, SF-3, SF-12, A-05): a `2026-07-28` session subscribes to its server's list changes — `serve` (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs`) opens a `subscriptions/listen` stream when the negotiated version has no `initialize`, asking for the list changes the server declares (`ChangeListener`, `declared_list_changes`), and a task forwards each onto the client's change channel as `ServerChange` through a weak sender, so the supervisor's §3.7 refresh hears a modern server; ## Part III — the modernization (§3.3)

Part III's landed ids (M01–M03, M44) are Part I's §3.1 rows; M63 is in Phase A–B, M04 + M07 landed as P36 (`0a0dea09`) in Phase L, and M08 is pre-written on the prep ref. plan-rev3 §5.4's and §5.5's after-P38 entries and SA-1…SA-4 belong to neither part and sit here with the steps that carry them. Steps 3a and 3b name no part; they follow step 3, *the first Part III source commit*, so they are listed here.

| Step 3b: N-ROUTE-1, N-TOOL-1b, N-TOOL-8, N-LEAD-6, N-LLM-1, SA-2, N-SKILL-5, N-EVENT-1; SA-DESIGN; SA-1, SA-3 | [§3.3 step 3b](tasks/deps-upgrade/programme-plan.md) | N-ROUTE-1: `fix(core): /pause and /resume say pause is unsupported and change no status` (plan-rev3 §5.5 N-ROUTE-1, the 2026-10-19 `/pause` row; Required). The chat's `/pause` and `/resume`, bare or with an id and whatever the lane holds, answer one sentence (`task_ops::PAUSE_UNSUPPORTED`: "Pause and resume are not supported — a workflow runs until it finishes or /cancel stops it.") and change nothing — no row, registry entry or lane status, no `TaskUpdated`. The guard sits in `handle_message_internal`'s task-ops match, before any target is resolved, so the turn is still a task op (`IntentClassified` until N-EVENT-1 deleted it, `OrchestrationStage` `task_ops`, the latency record, its attachments skipped); the intent parser and `/cancel` are unchanged. `POST /v1/tasks/{id}/action`'s `pause` and `resume` — the CLI's `openalpaca tasks pause` and `resume`, the Work view's Pause — and the experimental replay resume of an `interrupted` run, which the chat's `/resume` never reached, are unchanged. The Daemon and CLI Manuals, `docs/agent-loop.md` and the REPL's `/help` (`/cancel [id]`) say so, and the README's feature line no longer offers pause or resume. Learned from Claude Code (no pause: Esc interrupts a turn, `TaskStop` stops background work) and ZCode's run statuses (none paused), a design written fresh. N-TOOL-1b: `fix(core): one per-tool bound for every loop — 120 s by default, a 600 s ceiling, a model-settable timeout_secs clamped; a template's timeout_seconds no longer the per-tool bound` (plan-rev3 §5.5 N-TOOL-1b, §5.4's B-11 row, the 2026-10-19 row 1; Required). Every loop cuts a tool at `[execution.agent_defaults] max_tool_runtime_secs`, 60 → 120 s: the main loop per turn, the lead at its run's start, a subagent at its spawn, the file and plugin skill tiers per invocation, the nested tier per call (`SkillInvocationToolExecutor` carries the figures); `SandboxPolicy::from_constraints` takes the daemon's `AgentDefaults` and reads no `timeout_seconds`, and `LoopConfig.max_tool_runtime` is gone (the policy is the one carrier). A new key, `max_tool_runtime_ceiling_secs = 600` (1–3600, raised to the bound when set below it): `shell_execute` and every `Command`-backend tool may send `timeout_secs`, and the sandbox cuts the call at it — a larger figure at the ceiling, never refused (`SandboxPolicy::runtime_secs_for`) — naming the figure it cut at; a `Command`-backend tool is still also killed at its own TOML `timeout_secs`, whichever comes first, and a command the sandbox cuts below the most it may run — the ceiling, or that tool's own figure where smaller (`ToolRegistry::command_backend_timeout_secs`) — is told it may ask for more (`tools::platform::command_timed_out_hint`). Both skill tiers take the bound and the ceiling from one snapshot (`Orchestrator::tool_runtime_bounds`). `shell_execute` takes an optional integer `timeout_secs`, every loop's definition states the bound and the ceiling, and its own bound is 3600 s (was 300), the ceiling's highest setting. `[execution.lead_agent_defaults] max_tool_runtime_secs` is removed — the struct, its clamp, the shipped `config/daemon.toml`, the CLI mapping and the storage schema row (`daemon.execution.max_tool_runtime_ceiling_secs` takes its place) — and a `daemon.toml` still naming it boots with one WARN. `docs/agent-loop.md` and `docs/tools/DESIGN.md` and `TECHNICAL.md` say so. Learned from Claude Code (the Bash tool's per-call timeout, 120000 ms by default, 600000 at most) and ZCode's `bash-timeout-policy.ts`, a design written fresh. N-TOOL-8: `fix(core): a top-level JSON-string call decoded once at the sandbox's entry, before the sanitizer` (plan-rev3 §5.5 N-TOOL-8, pending-decisions §1.13's JSON-string row, the 2026-10-19 answer; Required). `SandboxManager::execute_tool`'s first step, `decoded_json_string_call`: a call whose arguments are a top-level JSON string whose text parses to an object is read as that object, once — a string inside it is never decoded, a string encoded twice holds no object and stays as sent — with one WARN naming the tool, the source (the agent the sandbox attributes the call to: a plugin agent's template id, `skill:<name>` for a plugin skill, the model's agent on the Anthropic path below) and the byte length, never the text; every later step reads the object — the capability check, the sanitizer, the registry's precheck and schema check, the approval cache's hash, the card (its preview, request and `ToolConfirmationRequested` event), the denial hook, the per-call bound and the tool. Its producers are a plugin agent's proxied call (`runner/plugin_agent.rs`) and a plugin skill's tool callback (`SandboxToolCallback`), each pinned by a test, and — found in review — Anthropic's non-streamed `parse_response` (`providers/anthropic/response.rs`), which passes a `tool_use`'s `input` as sent, never through `read_tool_arguments`: a string there only from a non-conforming server at `[providers.anthropic] base_url` (the Anthropic API sends an object), refused `got string` before, now read by the same decode. The registry still decodes nothing (N-TOOL-5's rule); `check_arguments`' doc, `docs/agent-loop.md`, `docs/tools/TECHNICAL.md` and CLAUDE.md's Gotchas line on where arguments are read say so (CLAUDE.md 3 bytes shorter). Decided by ZCode's input normalisation (`input-normalization.ts`, `call-runner.ts`), a design written fresh. N-LEAD-6: `fix(core): a run's subagents bounded by a lifetime backstop, refused with its number` (plan-rev3 §5.5 N-LEAD-6, pending-decisions §1.13's subagent queue bound, the 2026-10-19 answer; Required). A new key, `[execution.lead_agent_defaults] max_subagents_per_run = 48` — `max_concurrent_subagents`' 6 times the batch cap 8, a literal whose derivation a test checks against `spawn_subagents_batch`'s `maxItems` — clamped to 1–1000, with its storage schema row (`daemon.execution.lead_max_subagents_per_run`), its CLI mapping and a line in the shipped `config/daemon.toml`. `SpawnSubagentTool` reads it once, at the run's start, and takes a slot atomically after the self-spawn and depth guards, before `spawn_instance` (`reserve_spawn`): a run's next spawn past the bound — by `spawn_subagent`, or an entry of `spawn_subagents_batch`, whose earlier entries are spawned — is refused before anything is registered, naming the figure ("Maximum subagents per run (48) reached — this run has spawned 48, so no more can be spawned. …"); a spawn refused for another reason gives its slot back, and a resumed run's restored subagents (those its session log still records) count toward the bound. The lead prompt's step 3 names the figure the tool enforces (`build_lead_agent_prompt_from_templates` takes it; the golden updated). The concurrency cap and N-LEAD-2's queueing are unchanged; `docs/agent-loop.md`, `docs/tools/TECHNICAL.md` and `DESIGN.md` say so. Decided by Claude Code's form (a lifetime backstop far above normal use) and the lead's figure (ZCode has no bound by design), a design written fresh. N-LLM-1: `fix(llm,core): per-key concurrency 8, a full batch; the unused capacity estimate deleted; the lead told what queues a spawn` (plan-rev3 §5.5 N-LLM-1, pending-decisions §1.13's router per-key concurrency, the 2026-10-19 answer; Required). The router's `RateLimitConfig::per_key_concurrency` defaults to 8 (was 5) — a full batch, more than the lead's stock bound of six with the lead beside them — so a run alone on a one-key install no longer has its sixth subagent read `running` while its model call waits for one of its key's permits; a `[rate_limits]` table in `llm.toml` still sets its own. `LlmRouter::estimated_llm_capacity`, which nothing called, is deleted with its six tests, with `LlmCapacityInfo` (the public type it returned, re-exported by the crate) and with `KeyPool::available_api_key_count`, whose only caller it was, and that one's six tests; `docs/api` regenerated. The lead prompt's step 3, its `<tools>` line and `spawn_subagent`'s description no longer say spawning follows "LLM capacity": a subagent spawned past the run's concurrency bound is queued and starts when a slot frees, as the spawn's reply says (the golden updated, the description pinned whole). `docs/agent-loop.md` says a subagent whose model call waits inside the router — for the router's permit, its key's, the key's RPM and TPM budget or a cooldown — reads `running`, and that a watched chat turn streams and takes the router's permit alone, a key's too only on its non-streaming fallback. Decided by the lead (neither reference has a design for the mismatch). SA-2: `feat(core): the subagent objective is a self-contained brief — the tool descriptions and the lead prompt say so` (plan-rev3 §5.5 SA-2, pending-decisions §1.14's SA-2 row, the 2026-10-19 answer; Required). The lead prompt's `<workflow>` step 2 and both spawn tools' descriptions say each objective is written as a self-contained brief — the goal, the inputs it needs, the constraints, and what to return and where — and both tools' `objective` parameter (one text, `OBJECTIVE_DESCRIPTION`) says what each part holds and why: a subagent is given neither the user's request nor the lead's conversation, only its brief (the golden updated; the two descriptions and the parameter pinned whole); it names no length. No cut of an objective changes: the 500-character span row and `subagent_open` record and the 80-byte and 50-character log previews are records, unchanged, and a plugin-backed template's path is unchanged. A characterization pins, before and after, what a file-based template's subagent is given: the brief whole as its user turn, the last message, and — found in the tree, against the entry's premise — whole again as a second system message, the context package's task description (`ContextManager::distill`), which the spawn site does not leave out of Layer 3 as `build_subagent_minimal`'s doc asks; the composed system prompt holds neither. A second characterization pins what bounds a brief — found in the tree, against the entry's "Nothing caps it": on an 8,192-token model a 12,800-character brief reaches the subagent as its system copy alone, Layer 5's trim (`TRIM_THRESHOLD`, 75% of the window) having dropped the user turn, its only non-system message, which a brief counted twice passes at about 37.5% of the window; and a 20,000-character one as that copy cut to 40% of the window (`SUB_AGENT_CONTEXT_RATIO`; its first 13,104 characters) and marked `[...truncated]`. `docs/agent-loop.md` and `docs/tools/TECHNICAL.md` say so. Decided by Claude Code's Agent tool (the parent's prompt) and ZCode's `subagent.ts` (the prompt the child's first turn), a design written fresh. N-SKILL-5: `fix(skills): the catalog lists every skill's description — a 20,000-character budget, a 250-character cut` (plan-rev3 §5.5 N-SKILL-5, pending-decisions §1.13's `skills_catalog_budget` row, the 2026-10-19 answer; Required). `[orchestrator.prompt_budgets] skills_catalog_budget` defaults to 20,000 characters of skill lines (was 500), the top of its 100–20,000 range — `PromptBudgetsConfig`'s default, the shipped `config/daemon.toml`, and the storage schema row whose default `openalpaca config` shows (`daemon.orchestrator.skills_catalog_budget`, which the entry does not name) — and `<available_skills>` cuts a description to at most 250 characters, the ellipsis included (`AVAILABLE_SKILLS_DESCRIPTION_CHARS`, was 100); past the budget every skill is still listed by its id alone, none dropped. The four stock skills' block differs from the parent's in create-skill's line alone, its 199-character description now whole: 99 characters more (skill lines 398 → 497). The seeded template names no budget, so an install seeded from it reads the new default; a file that sets the key keeps its figure. `docs/agent-loop.md` says so. Decided by Claude Code (every description listed) within ZCode's ceiling (`skills.ts`: a 20,000-character budget, a 250-character cut, a names-only fallback), a design written fresh. N-EVENT-1: `refactor(core,daemon): the log-only event variants deleted with their arms` (plan-rev3 §5.5 N-EVENT-1, the 2026-10-19 telemetry row; Required). Five `SystemEvent` variants — published, logged at debug by the daemon's bridge, read by nothing — are gone with their publish sites and bridge arms: `IntentClassified`, `DispatchDecision`, `SkillDiscovered`, `SkillSelected` and `SkillContextInjected`; none had a `ServerEvent` twin, so no persistence arm, route, client or GUI home moves. What served them alone goes with them: the skill router's and the skill catalog's event bus and both `new_with_bus` constructors (the catalog's had no caller, so the daemon never published `SkillDiscovered`; the daemon builds its router with `SkillRouter::new`), and `Intent::intent_type`, which named an intent for `IntentClassified` only. `OrchestrationStage` stays under the entry's re-add clause, its consumers named on the variant (its list of modes given `memory_command` and `skill_withdrawn`, which the handler records and the list left out): the two live-eval harnesses and the orchestrator's tests read its `mode` (and `ack_ms`) off the bus, where the `orchestrator_latency` row has no lookup by request. The dispatch-decision record (`dispatch_decisions`, `GET /v1/orchestrator/decisions`), the latency record and `skill_execution_log` are unchanged. The Daemon Manual, the Skill Template Reference and `docs/agent-loop.md` say so; `docs/api` lists no event variant and is unchanged. Decided by the lead (the entry); no prior art borrowed. N-TOOL-1b's, the owner's, each built as no change: a template's `timeout_seconds` — every shipped template sets one, 120–600 s — now enforces nothing: enforce it as the task's wall clock (a new enforcement point), warn at boot that it is inert, or drop it from the shipped templates and the manuals (recommended: drop; Claude Code bounds no subagent by wall clock, and the shipped 300 s would stop most leads); a `Command`-backend tool's own TOML `timeout_secs` (1–300 s), which the entry does not name: read it as that tool's default, a call's `timeout_secs` lifting the backend's kill up to the ceiling — what "clamped to the ceiling" reads, reversing the documented bound (recommended) — or clamp such a call in the sandbox to it too (an enforcement point added), or keep what is built (it kills first, and the hint names it); `invoke_skill` exempt from the per-tool cut, as the nested tier's `invoke_skill:*` backends are (recommended: yes) — until then a skill the lead invokes stops at 120 s where its template gave 300. N-TOOL-8 leaves no decision to the owner (the entry built as the 2026-10-19 answer wrote it); for the lead, N-TOOL-5's residue: whether Anthropic's non-streamed `parse_response` should read a `tool_use`'s `input` through `read_tool_arguments_field`, as the stream collector and the OpenAI-compatible parser do (not `3d2f9e40`'s work). N-LEAD-6 leaves no decision to the owner (the entry built as the 2026-10-19 answer wrote it; its own choices — a resumed run's subagents counted (those its session log still records), a batch that crosses the bound accepted in part, the 1–1000 range, the CLI row, the figure read once per run — are in its body). N-LLM-1's, the owner's, built as no change: the per-key default also bounds a keyless local provider's synthetic slot (Ollama), so a local-model install now sends up to eight of the lead's and its subagents' unstreamed calls to its server at once where five, and a call the server queues past its own parallelism waits within the provider's request budget (600 s in the shipped template), where a call past the fifth waited in the router with no deadline — keep eight for the slot (built), give a keyless slot a figure of its own (an enforcement point added), or document it only (recommended: keep eight; an all-local install may set `[rate_limits] per_key_concurrency`). The entry is otherwise built as the 2026-10-19 answer wrote it; its own choices — `KeyPool::available_api_key_count` deleted with the estimate it alone served, the three sentences naming no figure, `global_concurrency`'s doc corrected from 4 to its default 10 — are in its body. SA-2's, the owner's, built as no change: a file-based template's subagent is given its objective twice — as its user turn and as a second system message, the context package's task description, which Anthropic's request joins to the system text and every compactor keeps as `messages[1]` — and its window bounds the brief: the copy is cut past 40% of it, and Layer 5's trim drops the user turn, the request's only non-system message, once the request passes 75%, which the second copy brings to about 37.5% of the window; past that the subagent has no user turn at all (an Anthropic request with an empty `messages`). So the 2026-10-19 row's "never its system prompt" and "Nothing caps it" do not hold, before SA-2 or after it: (a) leave the task description out of a spawned subagent's bundle, as `build_subagent_minimal`'s doc asks, with the compactors made to keep the objective's user turn in the copy's place and Layer 5's trim made never to take the current user turn (every loop's assembly; without it a brief past 75% of the window would reach the subagent in no form) — recommended: the row's own words, Claude Code's and ZCode's design, and a lead's objective can carry text read from a page or a file; its own commit, or SA-DESIGN's; (b) keep both copies and correct the row, the trim's drop left as it is; or (c) Phase 6's documented shape, the task description as a raw block at the system prompt's end (in the system prompt, against the row); until then a brief is given, and counted against the window, twice, which halves its room before its user turn is dropped. N-SKILL-5 leaves no decision to the owner (the entry built as the 2026-10-19 answer wrote it; its own choices — the storage schema row's default moved with the key's, the main loop's names-only test held at an explicit 500 — are in its body). N-EVENT-1 leaves no decision to the owner (the entry built as the 2026-10-19 answer wrote it; its own choices — `OrchestrationStage` kept for its consumers; the router's and the catalog's bus, both `new_with_bus` and `Intent::intent_type` deleted with the events they alone served — are in its body); the bridge's eight other log-only arms, variants the entry does not name (`ModelAccessDenied`, `UserProfileUpdated`, `IdentityUpdated`, `BootstrapCompleted`, `ContextBudgetComputed`, `CompactionTriggered`, `ComposeLayerCacheHit`, `ComposeLayerCacheMiss`), are as they were. |

## How this file is kept

- Every landing updates its row in the same commit: the status, the evidence with the commit hash and the figures its `Verified:` line names, and the open items it closes or opens. A run of record is updated by the commit that records its result.
- The lead keeps this file. The plan of record stays `tasks/deps-upgrade/programme-plan.md`; where the two differ, the plan wins and this file is corrected in the next commit.
- A row is Complete only when every entry in it has landed and its open items are done. An open item that is an owner action (§5) does not keep a landed row from Complete; it stays listed in the row and in *Owner actions still open*. A part's row in *The parts* closes only when every row of that part is Complete and its own open items are done.
- A phase whose test pin landed with Phase 0 (F's P14, H's P24) stays Not started until its first remaining entry lands; Phase C, whose one entry is P05, is Complete. An entry the plan drops, defers or supersedes takes no status word: it is named, with the plan's word, in the line under Part I's tables.
- Dates and times are UTC, each beside the commit hash that carries it.
